I don't feel much of anything when it happens. It's just... a blank.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after something very frightening or upsetting happened to you?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. Nothing frightening happened. It just... started.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you also forget how to do familiar everyday tasks?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I can still do things at home just fine. It's mostly just... what I already told you.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend a lot of time worrying about many different things in your life?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't... worry that much. I just... I don't know. No.\n\nDoctor: Have you been forgetting recent conversations?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. If someone talks to me, I remember it. It hasn't really been a problem.\n\nDoctor: When this happens, is it that you cannot remember how to do the work task?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess. It's just... my mind goes blank. Like I'll be looking at the road-closure forms, or... the stop-slow paddle. And I suddenly can't remember the steps. Things I've done for years. It just... goes blank.\n\nDoctor: Are the same work tasks always the ones that go blank for you?\n\nPatient: I don't really know... I haven't noticed if it's always the same ones. It just... happens. I haven't really kept track.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank at work, can you still remember what the task is for, even if you cannot remember the steps to do it?\n\nPatient: I don't... I'm not really sure. I guess I know I'm supposed to be working, but... I don't know. I haven't really thought about it like that. It's just... everything kind of stops.\n\nDoctor: When this happens, do you still recognize the tools or forms in front of you?\n\nPatient: I... yeah, I know what they are. I recognize the paddle. But... like I said, I'll be looking right at the forms or the paddle, and... I just can't remember what to do with them. The steps are just gone.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank at work, do you still know what day it is?\n\nPatient: I... I guess so. I'm not really thinking about what day it is when that happens. I just... I don't know. I haven't really paid attention to that.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank at work, do you know what day it is?\n\nPatient: I... didn't you just ask me that? Like I said... I don't really know. I'm not thinking about the day of the week when it happens. I just... I don't know.\n\nDoctor: Outside of those moments at work, do you have trouble learning new things?\n\nPatient: I... I don't think so. I haven't really tried to learn anything new lately. I just... do my job and go home.\n\nDoctor: When this happens, do you still remember what the next step should be if someone tells you to do it?\n\nPatient: I... I suppose so. If someone tells me what to do, I can usually follow along. I just... I don't really think about it. I just try to get through the shift.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever suddenly forgotten how to do another familiar skill, like driving or making tea?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I can still drive to my spot just fine. I haven't really noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank at work, do you still remember your coworkers’ names?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I know their names. It's just... the tasks that slip away. Not the people.\n\nDoctor: After someone reminds you of the steps at work, do you remember them later on your own?\n\nPatient: Not always... no. It just keeps happening. That's... why I came in, actually. My mind just keeps going blank at the worst times.
